# Phase 1-3 Implementation Traceability Matrix

Audit date: 2026-11-24. Branch worktree of `fix/p0-ai-cost-fixes` at `021f0a47`. Migrations audited through `20261113300000`; this change adds `20261124000000` to `20261124300000`.

Sources (read in full, as text, from the supplied PDFs): the 13 PDFs in `Phase 1 documents/`, the 4 in `phase 2 documents/`, the 3 in `phase 3/`; the 7 in `phase 4/` are in `docs/phase-4-implementation-traceability.md` (its row-level section was refreshed in the same change) and the admin panel PDF is in `docs/admin-panel-screen-traceability.md` (its stale rows were corrected: 71 of 71 screens EXISTS; GST-portal filing is the one MANUAL_EXTERNAL remainder).

## How to read this

- One row per concrete requirement, entity, event, state, rule, screen or test-case group. Some acceptance-criteria rows group several tiny items; no document section is skipped.
- **EXISTS** means a door, handler, page or rule exists and the evidence column names it. **PARTIAL** means part of the requirement is built; the evidence says what is absent. **MISSING** means nothing was found. **MANUAL_EXTERNAL** means it needs a funded model, WhatsApp/email/payment-provider credentials, a real third-party account or an owner decision, and is left honest rather than faked.
- Evidence strength: a `verify-*.mjs` / `verify-*.sql` script that drives a real Postgres is stronger than a `tests/*.test.ts` file, which is usually a regex over source. The row says which where the auditors recorded it.
- **The audit was read-only for the rows first written by the six audit passes: those auditors read and grepped the code and did not run the existing verifiers.** Only the rows marked "Closed 2026-11-24" were built and run in this change, with the evidence named in the row.
- `BUILDABLE` in an open row means it can be built without external credentials; `EXTERNAL(...)` names what is missing. Many PARTIAL rows are PARTIAL only because live provider proof needs credentials; they say so.
- Corrections to earlier docs found on the way: `ai.routing_policies` has readers now; the M1/M2 milestone-position bug and the payment-verification grant bug of the 2026-09-28 audit are fixed; the dead Phase 3 revision loop is now answered (P3-UID-015); Scheduler, Quotation Master and Coordination are services, not registry agents, by design (the owner call whether the five-agent framing is binding is open: ADM-82); P1-FLOW-063's "only the discount is enforced" was wrong (minimum price, autonomous ceiling and round limit are enforced).

Every SQL control above was red-proved: the live function definition was mutated through `pg_get_functiondef` (a mutation that changed nothing raised `NO-OP MUTATION`) and the verifier had to go red, then the migration was re-applied and the verifier went green. Counts of checks and red-proofs are in the row evidence. The TypeScript controls were red-proved by editing the source and watching the named test fail.

## What stays open, and why

- **Needs a funded model:** real-model quality of every agent output (planning blueprint, design directions and revisions, meeting analysis, quote drafting, QA findings); the Phase 1 V1-V5 real-model scenario; non-Anthropic tool calling. Everything agent-shaped here is proved against a stand-in model only.
- **Needs provider credentials:** the production WhatsApp number and templates (BLK-003) for every live send including the new meeting announcements; email provider and SPF/DKIM; Google Calendar service account (BLK-005); Figma write (MANUAL_FIGMA_REQUIRED); Apple/Google signing for APK/iOS prototype outputs; a payment gateway (none by design: an Admin verifies by hand); GST-portal filing; a speech provider for voice notes.
- **Owner decisions, not code:** whether the five-agent framing (Scheduler, Quotation Master, Coordination as registry agents) is binding; whether PM milestone messages go straight to the client rather than the internal channel; approval by WhatsApp reply (ADM-74); the 14-state lead vocabulary; role names (Auditor etc.); ROUTE_SCORE weights; editing client-facing wording; the Hindi templates.
- **Buildable and still open** (about 290 PARTIAL/MISSING rows carry BUILDABLE; the largest clusters): scheduling policy as organization settings (notice, buffer, working hours, proposal expiry, contact timezone); agent-led scheduling in chat; acceptance evidence fields on the accepted quote; the handoff envelope columns (acceptance criteria, retry budget, idempotency) and a task lifecycle for `ai.handoffs`; a workflow task board and negotiation workspace page; policy versioning for approvals; Phase 4 prototype QA depth (intake/blocked outcomes, defect close on fix, defect rows, render smoke check); gating the designer's client-revision job on the PM classification; the Phase 2 governed exception for planning before the advance is verified; planner events; credit notes. They were not built in this change; each row says what to build.
- **Known limits of what was built:** the READY_FOR_TASK3 branch of the PM state needs a verified M2 payment fixture and is not exercised by its verifier; the quotation comparison page and the Task 2 state component were type-checked and linted but not rendered in a browser; the rate limiter is a fixed window and does not cover the landing-page route; the meeting announcement carries 4 of the 13 fields P1-SCHED-030 lists; a revised theme direction keeps its option index and so the option list shows both versions until a UI filter is added.

## Phase 1 - Coordination Agent

| ID | Source | Requirement | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| P1-COORD-001 | s1-3 mission and boundaries | Coordination is a workflow-handoff layer that never replaces Sales, Scheduler, Quotation, Orchestrator or Admin authority | PARTIAL | There is no `coordination` agent in src/modules/agents/registry.ts (by design, the documented architecture: coordination is spread across ai.handoffs (20260814120003), core.jobs/outbox, the phase tables and the PM agent). The behaviour boundary holds (sales has no acceptance/pricing tool; verify-authority-guards.mjs). The agent identity does not exist. OWNER decision (ADM-82) whether the five-agent framing is binding |
| P1-COORD-002 | s4 coordination map | Each stage (lead, conversation, meeting, requirements, quote, approval, delivery, negotiation, acceptance, payment gate, WON) has a coordination duty | PARTIAL | Each hop exists as a door or handler (crm.ingest, sales drafts, subtask_requests, approvals, send gate, record_proposal_response, WON gate, won_handoff_packet); no single coordination task object ties them. Evidence: verify-journey.mjs, verify-flow-01.mjs, verify-won-gate.mjs, verify-won-handoff.mjs (live) |
| P1-COORD-003 | s5 core task contract | A durable task with id, tenant, scope ids, source/destination, type, state, version refs, acceptance criteria, priority/deadline, policy refs, evidence, blockers, retry count, correlation and idempotency key | EXISTS | **Closed 2026-11-27 (p1o):** migration `20261127000000`, `scripts/verify-p1o-handoffs.sql` (live checks, red-proofs): `ai.handoffs` now carries acceptance_criteria, priority, retry_count, previous_failure_summary, idempotency_key, policy_decision_ref/policy_version, approval_request_id, dependency_ids, blocker; `ai.p1o_create_handoff` is idempotent on the key and refuses a task with no criteria; `src/modules/orchestrator/p1o-envelope.ts` validates the envelope at intake. Earlier audit finding: ai.handoffs has id, organization_id, correlation_id, from/to agent, status, project/subject refs, objective, context, requirements, artifacts, constraints, verification, unresolved, sla_at, depth.
